The Kremlin spokesperson, Dmitry Peskov, has said Russia rejects the idea of a temporary ceasefire, telling reporters that Moscow wanted a long-term peace deal instead.
“In the Kremlin, both ‌President Putin and officials at other levels have repeatedly stated that ​we do not want a ceasefire,” said Peskov. “We want long-term peace and are prepared to do everything possible ⁠to achieve it.“
Peskov’s comments were a rejection of a ⁠60-day ceasefire idea proposed by Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy ⁠on Sunday to allow ⁠his ​country to hold presidential elections. Kyiv has accused Russia of violating temporary truces when ceasefires between the two sides have been agreed to in the past.
Peskov was asked to comment on Trump’s self-declared “energy ceasefire” between Russia and Ukraine and refused to be drawn on it, only saying:
Contacts continue. As soon as we have something to say on the matter, we will inform you.

⁠Ukrainian president Volodymyr Zelenskyy said ⁠it ⁠was “increasingly clear” ​that Russia ⁠was unwilling to accept a US-proposed ⁠ceasefire ​on ‌energy ‌strikes, as Moscow hit ‌six Ukrainian energy facilities on Monday.
“This is ‌what happens when Russia ​sees the prospect of higher revenues ⁠for its war ​machine,” ​he wrote ​in ​a ‌post ​on X.
Russia presents a long-term threat to Europe regardless of the outcome of the war in Ukraine , the head of Germany’s domestic intelligence agency said.
Warning that Moscow wanted to upend Europe’s international order, Sina Sellen, president of the BfV, said Russian-orchestrated attacks would “continue to come”.
“The hybrid threat is here, and it has long been decoupled from the situation in Ukraine,” he said at a Berlin conference organised by the BfV.
“For Russia, [the goal] is a fundamentally new map of Europe and of its role within Europe and in geopolitics.”
A 13-year-old girl was among three people killed in a Russian strike on a house near the frontline city of Kramatorsk in eastern Ukraine on Monday, the authorities said.
The head of the Donetsk regional military administration, Vadym Filashkin, said a house in the Cherkaske area was hit with cluster munitions, accusing Russia of “a deliberate attempt” to maximise civilian casualties.
German chancellor Friedrich Merz said that Russia would only negotiate “seriously” under pressure, days after president Donald Trump announced a temporary easing of US sanctions on Russian diesel.
“For Germany and for Europe, such a decision is out of the question,” said Merz, referring to the US-Russia diesel deal.
“Russia will only negotiate seriously under pressure and that is why we are maintaining our sanctions against Russia.”
Nato should signal its “red lines” to Russia over sabotage attacks in Europe, the alliance’s top military commander said.
“It’s important that allies continue to consult, think about how we might respond, and make sure that we message clearly and concisely to the Russians where our red lines are,” Nato commander Alexus Grynkewich said in Warsaw.
Ukrainian officials said Russian attacks killed six people in two Ukrainian cities yesterday.
In Kharkiv, Ukraine’s second-largest city, a strike on a grocery store killed three people and injured 20 others, including two children, the regional governor, Oleh Syniehubov, said.
Footage released by the Kharkiv prosecutor’s office captured the moment of the strike, showing debris flying through the air and fires breaking out as car alarms sounded.
Huge explosion captured on CCTV after deadly strike hits grocery store in Kharkiv – video
An “unprecedented” cyber-attack is behind the shutdown of refrigerator units in multiple French supermarkets, according to the chief executive of Cooperative U, one of the chains affected.
At least 150 Intermarche shops were targeted, along with almost 100 Cooperative U outlets, AFP reports. Paris prosecutors confirmed the attack, with media reporting that hackers were “managing to remotely trigger the forced defrosting of the refrigeration systems in numerous stores.”
Reuters is carrying some further quotes from Nato’s supreme allied commander for Europe , Gen Alexus Grynkewich , about how the alliance says it would deal with an attack on Nato territory. He told the audience at the Warsaw Security Forum:
If someone ​is foolish enough to attack us, we will fight. We will stop the attack and deny them the ability to achieve their objectives, whatever those might be. We will, in short, ensure any attack, against any ally, ⁠proves utterly futile.
Should the adversary ​choose to press ‌the attack ​or commit his reserves, ​we will break the warfighting system engaged against us. This includes not just the attacking force itself, but anything and everything that supports, sustains, and controls that force.
The US general is speaking just days after president Donald Trump announced a deal to buy Russian diesel .
Nato’s supreme allied commander for Europe , Gen Alexus Grynkewich has been outlining what he describes as the Nato “theory of victory”, in which he says Nato forces would block, then blunt, then break any aggressive move against the alliance.
While stressing that he did not see conflict as “inevitable”, he said at the Warsaw Security Forum it was a plan to “prepare us to execute our most sacred task – the defence of our people and our territory.”
Nato’s supreme allied commander for Europe , Gen Alexus Grynkewich , is speaking now at the Warsaw Security Forum. We will bring you any key lines that emerge.
Nato’s top commander Grynkewich addresses Warsaw security forum – watch live
A little more from Kremlin spokesperson Dmitry Peskov here on the prospects of a limited truce agreement. He says that Russia would potentially offer a one-day truce if Ukraine were to hold elections. He said:
If they truly want to hold elections in Kyiv, then the president of Russia, the commander-in-chief, will be ready to give an order not to launch strikes on Ukraine on election day, specifically on election day.
France’s high school students will stage new protests on Tuesday, the Agence France-Presse (AFP) news agency is reporting,
A government source told journalists last week that the cost for damage caused by the demonstrations, which have shaken an unpopular government trying to reduce a ballooning budget deficit, was already “at least 30 million euros”.
Widespread disillusionment has seen young people protest over recent weeks against what they say is underfunding and neglect of the education system, with the demonstrations marred by often violent clashes that have injured hundreds.
Trying to appease the student’s demands, the government announced last week that about 3,000 substitute teachers would be deployed in the coming days to replace absent staff at the 114 worst-affected high schools.
But the Union of High School students described the initial announcements as “crumbs” and maintain their demand for an extra €10m (£8.4m) to be invested in the country’s lycees, where students – most between 15 and 18 – complete their high school education.
On Friday, the government noted a drop in the number of blockades and meetings outside the country’s lycees. More than 96% were opening on Friday, the education ministry said, but now a new stage of the campaign for improved conditions could mark a turning point in the protests.
Protesters and high-school students take part in a demonstration outside the Lycee Toulouse Lautrec, in Toulouse on 12 OCtober 2026. Photograph: Ed Jones/AFP/Getty Images

### 4. ★★★★☆ Dow, S&P 500, Nasdaq Futures Climb After Oil In Focus As Trump Declares ‘Energy Ceasefire’ Between Russia And Ukraine: IREN, WFF, VSTM, CIFR Stocks In Focus - Yahoo Finance

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: GN Markets / Al Jazeera
- **多源确认**: 2
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T03:50:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Dow, S&amp;P 500, Nasdaq Futures Climb After Oil In Focus As Trump Declares ‘Energy Ceasefire’ Between Russia And Ukraine: IREN, WFF, VSTM, CIFR Stocks In Focus&nbsp;&nbsp;Yahoo Finance

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Dow, S&P 500, Nasdaq Futures Climb After Oil In Focus As Trump Declares ‘Energy Ceasefire’ Between Russia And Ukraine: IREN, WFF, VSTM, CIFR Stocks In Focus
On Friday, Trump said Russian leader Vladimir Putin had agreed to supply diesel to America and the global markets following a discussion
However, the deal with Russia was met with backlash from Republican and Democratic lawmakers, who said that it violates U.S. laws.
The telecom sector was also in focus after SpaceX said last week that it had agreed to buy up to 14 megahertz of paired spectrum in the 800 MHz band, putting Starlink Mobile on track to compete as a major U.S. mobile carrier.
U.S. stock futures edged higher late Sunday after a drop in oil prices, following U.S. President Donald Trump's announcement that Russia and Ukraine have agreed to an "energy ceasefire" effective immediately.
As of 11:27 p.m. EDT, Dow futures were up about 0.34%, the S&P 500 futures gained 0.31% and the Nasdaq-100 futures climbed 0.43%.
See what 10M+ investors are talking about. Get the Stocktwits Daily Rip for what retail is watching right now, free to your inbox
On Friday, the three benchmark indexes closed higher . The Dow Jones Industrial Average led the climb, adding more than 400 points to close up 0.83%. The S&P 500 gained 0.59% at close, while the Nasdaq Composite was up 0.64% at the end of the session.
Last week, all three U.S. benchmark indexes closed higher.
koyfin_20261012_083501091.png Key US Market Drivers
Oil was in focus late Sunday amid the dualities of an energy ceasefire announced between Russia and Ukraine and continued Middle East conflict, which kept prices above $100 a barrel.
Trump announced in a Truth Social post, "Effective immediately, there is an ENERGY CEASEFIRE in the War between Russia and Ukraine. Both parties have agreed. Do not break it!"
Earlier on Friday, Trump said that Russian leader Vladimir Putin had agreed to supply diesel to America and the global markets following a discussion. "Russia will immediately supply over 300,000 Tons of Diesel Fuel to the American and Global Marketplace, another 500,000 Tons during the month of November, and 1,000,000 Tons immediately thereafter. Additionally, based on the condition of their Diesel Refineries, Russia will then deliver, within a short period of time, 3,000,000 Tons of Diesel Fuel," Trump said.
Oil prices edged lower at the time of writing, although they continued to trade over $100 a barrel. Brent crude futures expiring in December were trading at $102.91 a barrel, down nearly 2%, while WTI crude futures expiring in November traded at $90.03 per barrel.
However, the deal with Russia was met with backlash from Republican and Democratic lawmakers, who said that it violates U.S. laws.
On Sunday, a group of seven senators, including four Republicans and three Democrats, released a statement urging the administration to "immediately reverse course."
"Congress explicitly banned Russian energy imports in 2022 to prevent American dollars from financing Putin's brutal war against Ukraine. Congress was clear: the U.S. should not import oil from Russia until it ends its aggression against Ukraine and ceases threats against our NATO Allies. President Trump's decision to allow Russian diesel imports to the U.S. runs directly counter to that law," they said.
"The United States should not provide Putin with a financial lifeline while Russia continues to kill Ukrainian civilians," they added.
Meanwhile, conflict in the Middle East continued to escalate over the weekend after the Houthis claimed ‌responsibility ⁠for a Saturday strike on Riyadh's international airport that killed 13 people and wounded more than 300.
Additionally, BP withdrew from the World Petroleum Council Energy Congress in Riyadh on Sunday after the attack.
"We made every effort to participate in WPC. ‌Unfortunately, ⁠due to travel disruption, we are unable to be there in person. We greatly value our longstanding partnerships across the region," a BP spokesperson ​reportedly said, as per Reuters.
The telecom sector was also in focus after SpaceX (SPCX) said it had agreed to buy up to 14 megahertz of paired spectrum in the 800 MHz band, putting Starlink Mobile on track to compete as a major U.S. mobile carrier.
Shares of other telecom companies including AT&T Inc. (T), T-Mobile US (TMUS), Verizon Inc. (VZ) and Comcast (CMCSA) sank following the announcement.
Meanwhile, investors are gearing up for the third-quarter earnings season, which starts next week with major U.S. banks reporting results.
IREN Ltd. (IREN): Shares of the company jumped 9% in the overnight session late Sunday after a media report indicated that Anthropic is the mystery AI cloud customer that IREN had referenced in its earnings call a few months ago.
WF Holding Limited (WFF): The microcap company drew retail attention after its shares pulled back overnight after jumping more than 499% on Friday amid speculative trading.
Verastem Inc. (VSTM): The biopharmaceutical company was in the spotlight ahead of its investor event on Wednesday, where it is expected to present initial efficacy results and updated safety data for its oral cancer drug.
Cipher Digital Inc. (CIFR): The company's shares moved 2% higher in the overnight session on Sunday, riding in sympathy with peer IREN's steep climb higher.
Among ETFs tracking benchmark indexes, the SPDR S&P 500 ETF (SPY), the SPDR Dow Jones Industrial Average ETF Trust (DIA) and the Invesco QQQ Trust (QQQ) all climbed at the time of writing, with sentiment in the 'bullish' territory.
Meanwhile, U.S. long-dated Treasury yields continued above 5%. At the time of writing, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield was trading at 5.242%, and the 30-year Treasury bond yield was 5.60%.
The iShares 20+ Year Treasury Bond ETF (TLT) was up 0.18% amid 'bullish' sentiment.
Asian markets opened mixed on Monday. South Korea's KOSPI, Japan's Nikkei 225 and China's SSE Composite were down at the time of writing, while Australian stocks were trading higher at the open.

Two More Oil Tankers Attacked Near Strait of Hormuz
Two more tankers were attacked near the Strait of Hormuz on Sunday, one in Hormuz and the other off the coast of Oman, Argus has reported , citing ship-tracking data and the Indian shipping minister.
One of the targets was a Suezmax loaded with Iraqi crude, which had set sail on October 8, the Indian top shipping official said. It was hit by an unknown projectile that caused fire on board and, at the time of writing, two sailors were still missing . The other tanker was a product carrier that was hit by a drone. That attack was reported earlier by the UK Maritime Trade Operations outlet.
"India reiterates the need for immediate de-escalation and a resolution to the ongoing conflict through dialogue and diplomacy," New Delhi's foreign ministry said. "Attacks on commercial vessels, seafarers and civilian infrastructure must cease. Freedom of navigation and the safe, unimpeded flow of maritime commerce through international waterways, in accordance with international law, must be upheld."
Iran's Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps has stepped up attacks on tankers in the Strait of Hormuz in the past couple of weeks, targeting vessels trying to transit the strait in dark mode, along a southern corridor near the coast of Oman.
The U.S. Navy is meanwhile striking Iranian-linked vessels, with the strikes heightening the already considerable shipping risk in the area. "All ships within the limits of the Strait of Hormuz that turn off their navigation and identification systems will be identified as hostile and will be dealt with severely and decisively," the IRGC's Navy forces said in a statement, as reported by Iranian state media.
The news of the latest attacks comes alongside a report about a so-called energy ceasefire between Russia and Ukraine, announced by President Trump over the weekend. Neither of the warring parties has confirmed the ceasefire, and Axios has since reported that it is a narrower refinery-only ceasefire proposal, but the news has pressured oil prices. Brent was trading above $103 per barrel after the news, and WTI was over $90 per barrel.

### 10. ★★★☆☆ Ukraine’s Zelenskyy accuses Russia of violating Trump’s ‘energy ceasefire’

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: Al Jazeera
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T10:35:51+00:00
- **发生了什么**: The EU also announced that it officially added almost 1,600 new sanctions on Russia&#039;s military industrial complex.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Share Ukraine’s Zelenskyy accuses Russia of violating Trump’s ‘energy ceasefire’ on social media
Ukrainian leader Volodymyr Zelenskyy speaks to the audience via an online video call during the opening ceremony at the Warsaw Security Forum on October 12, 2026, in Warsaw, Poland [Omar Marques/Getty Images]
Ukraine’s Volodymyr Zelenskyy has accused Russia of violating the “energy ceasefire” that United States President Donald Trump unilaterally announced at the weekend and promised to respond if the attacks continue.
Speaking at the Warsaw Security Forum via videolink on Monday, the Ukrainian president urged the US to step in to ensure ⁠that Moscow abided by the truce, noting that there have already been “Russian violations of the energy ceasefire announced by ‌President Trump.”
list of 4 items list 1 of 4 Trump calls on Ukraine to replace Zelenskyy amid tensions over Russia deal
list 2 of 4 Russia’s Yandex says data centre shut down after drone attack
list 3 of 4 Oval Office spat to public rebukes: A timeline of Trump-Zelenskyy ties
list 4 of 4 Zelenskyy says Russia violated energy truce announced by Trump
“We are waiting for the details of the conditions between the Americans and Russians, ⁠and we are waiting ⁠to see what specific steps the Americans are ready to take,” said Zelenskyy, who expressed surprise at the ceasefire deal.
“This ⁠energy ceasefire, it can work, and it can become a major step toward ending ‌the war if the Americans truly step in now and make ‌sure ‌Russians respect it … If Russia does not agree to an energy ceasefire, we will respond,” Zelenskyy told the Warsaw Security Forum.
Trump’s announcement on Sunday that the two countries had agreed to an “energy ceasefire” caused confusion. It came two days after Trump declared a temporary easing of US sanctions on Russian diesel, prompting outrage from European allies and Kyiv, who said it would only embolden Russian President Vladimir Putin.
Both Ukraine and Russia have been launching mutual attacks on each other’s energy infrastructure for months, adding to upward pressure on global energy prices that have risen amid the US-Israeli war on Iran.
Zelenskyy initially denied any knowledge of the agreement and said it was “news to him,” according to Axios.
Trump has previously taken to his Truth Social account to announce truces or other arrangements before either Kyiv or Moscow confirmed them.
“An energy ceasefire and other steps to reduce escalation and guarantee security are truly needed here in reality, not just on Truth Social,”  Zelenskyy added.
European leaders expressed encouragement at the proposed ceasefire, but agreed with Zelenskyy that it had not taken effect.
“I have not noted, over‌ the last 48 hours, any energy ceasefire coming from the ‌Russian ‌side,” French President Emmanuel Macron told reporters at a Paris autoshow.
At the Warsaw Security Forum, ⁠Polish Prime Minister Donald Tusk said that “Russia is not interested in ‌a ceasefire, de-escalation, or peace … that is why I will go to Brussels to urge all European partners ⁠to consider increasing ⁠sanctions.”
In Brussels, the European Union announced that it has officially added almost 1,600 new sanctions on Russia’s military industrial complex, including individuals and entities involved in its four-and-a-half-year war , in a bid to maintain pressure on Moscow.
“This is the single largest expansion of our sanctions list since Russia started its war,” EU foreign policy chief Kaja Kallas wrote online. “Europe is again showing a united front … We are making it significantly harder, slower and more expensive for Russia to produce the weapons it uses to kill Ukrainians,” she added.
Meanwhile, Russia ⁠attacked Ukrainian gas production facilities belonging to its state ⁠oil and gas ⁠company Naftogaz in the Kharkiv region overnight, which caused a fire, the ‌company said on Monday.
“The attack damaged critical equipment. Our employees are doing everything possible ⁠to repair the ⁠damage as quickly as possible and ⁠prevent risks to ⁠the stable ⁠operation of the energy system,” Naftogaz acting CEO ‌Sergii Fedorenko wrote on Telegram.
A day earlier, Zelenskyy had said that Ukraine was ready to halt attacks on Russian oil refineries if Moscow ceased its own attacks on Ukrainian energy infrastructure.

Seafarer's union official says crews offered big money to transit Strait of Hormuz As attacks continue to mount on shipping vessels transiting the Strait of Hormuz, a seafarer's union official says some crews are being offered big rewards for making the crossing.
Seafarer's union official says crews offered big money to transit Strait of Hormuz
Seafarer's union official says crews offered big money to transit Strait of Hormuz
< iframe src="https://www.npr.org/player/embed/nx-s1-5995186/nx-s1-9960629" width="100%" height="290" frameborder="0" scrolling="no" title="NPR embedded audio player">
As attacks continue to mount on shipping vessels transiting the Strait of Hormuz, a seafarer's union official says some crews are being offered big rewards for making the crossing.
JOEL ROSE, HOST: President Trump blames the Russia-Ukraine war for soaring energy costs. The war with Iran, though, is the dominant factor keeping global diesel prices high. This weekend, the U.K. Maritime Trade Operations Centre reported several oil tankers traversing the Strait of Hormuz were attacked. Nearly a hundred vessels have been hit since the U.S. war with Iran began, resulting in two dozen deaths, according to the U.N. And the attacks are taking on new forms. MANOJ YADAV: Small boats that surrounded a few of the vessels and started firing with RPG and even with the guns. ROSE: Manoj Yadav is the general secretary of a union representing merchant sailors from India - many of whom work in the Persian Gulf. He says because of that danger, ship captains and sailors making these transits are being offered big money. YADAV: Not only one time, two time, three times - five times of their salary they are getting just to transit the Hormuz Strait. ROSE: Five times their regular salary. YADAV: Yeah. ROSE: And if they don't comply, Yadav says they might be punished. YADAV: And maybe there are possibility that they will get blacklisted. ROSE: Now, oil deliveries through the Strait of Hormuz have recently seen a recovery, maybe as much as 80% of the pre-war supply, according to trade intelligence firm Kpler. But the cost of getting through the choke point continues to hit pocketbooks around the world. And in the U.S., that's raising the political stakes ahead of the midterm elections.

US Energy Secretary: Oil Flows Through Hormuz Reach 80-90% of Pre-Conflict Levels
https://english.aawsat.com/business/5328610-us-energy-secretary-oil-flows-through-hormuz-reach-80-90-pre-conflict-levels
US Energy Secretary: Oil Flows Through Hormuz Reach 80-90% of Pre-Conflict Levels
Wright to WPC Energy Congress: Refined product flows still at 25-35%
US Energy Secretary Chris Wright participating via video call in the WPC Energy Congress. (Asharq Al-Awsat)
11:01-12 October 2026 AD ـ 02 Jumada Al-Ula 1448 AH
11:01-12 October 2026 AD ـ 02 Jumada Al-Ula 1448 AH
US Energy Secretary: Oil Flows Through Hormuz Reach 80-90% of Pre-Conflict Levels
US Energy Secretary Chris Wright participating via video call in the WPC Energy Congress. (Asharq Al-Awsat)
US Energy Secretary Chris Wright said oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz had recovered to 80% to 90% of pre-conflict levels, averaging 13 million to 14 million barrels per day, while increased flows through alternative pipelines had helped support global supplies.
Speaking via video link at the WPC Energy Congress, Wright said spare production capacity and substantial inventories had helped keep crude oil supplies at manageable levels despite ongoing challenges. The crude oil supply problem was largely under control, but not fully resolved, he said.
The bigger challenge was refined petroleum product flows out of the Arabian Gulf, which remained at just 25% to 35% of pre-conflict levels, worsening shortages of diesel, jet fuel and gasoline.
Wright said the decline in Gulf refined product exports coincided with lower supplies from other sources. Russia, once a major diesel supplier, had become a net importer, albeit in limited volumes.
China had sharply cut refined product exports and announced it would not export such products in October, adding to pressure on global markets, he said.
Wright expressed hope that increased output from Chinese refineries, which have spare capacity and are benefiting from attractive profit margins, would help ease shortages. US refineries were also processing crude and producing refined products at record levels, he added.
He said the course of the Russia-Ukraine war and China's policy on refined product exports would affect supply in the coming period.
Wright said maritime shipping was another factor complicating the recovery in refined product flows from the Gulf, citing higher transport costs and longer shipping routes.
Houthi disruptions in the Bab al-Mandab Strait had forced oil tankers to take longer routes, while shifts in supply sources and Iranian attacks on vessels had added pressure on available shipping capacity, he said.
A decline in Iranian, Venezuelan and Russian oil flows through channels that had used the so-called “dark fleet” had freed up a significant number of tankers previously used to transport those supplies, Wright added.
Some of those vessels needed to be reintegrated into the mainstream shipping system, with sanctions lifted where possible, to increase capacity for transporting refined products from the Gulf and support continued oil and gas operations, he said.
Wright warned that higher shipping costs were having a greater impact on markets farther from supply sources. Global diesel prices had reached very high levels, while jet fuel prices had also risen, alongside a more moderate increase in gasoline prices.
Spare production capacity and inventories had helped contain pressure on the crude oil market, he said, but shortages of refined products and constraints on maritime shipping continued to pose challenges.
Wright concluded that the response to the conflict had demonstrated the energy system's ability to adapt, but stressed that the crisis was not over and that continued efforts were needed to maintain stable supplies.
Aramco President & CEO: Attacks on the Region’s Energy Infrastructure Threaten Global Economies
https://english.aawsat.com/business/5328676-aramco-president-ceo-attacks-region%E2%80%99s-energy-infrastructure-threaten-global
Aramco President & CEO: Attacks on the Region’s Energy Infrastructure Threaten Global Economies
Amin Nasser speaks at the 25th WPC Energy Congress in Riyadh (Asharq Al-Awsat)
13:47-12 October 2026 AD ـ 02 Jumada Al-Ula 1448 AH
13:47-12 October 2026 AD ـ 02 Jumada Al-Ula 1448 AH
Aramco President & CEO: Attacks on the Region’s Energy Infrastructure Threaten Global Economies
Amin Nasser speaks at the 25th WPC Energy Congress in Riyadh (Asharq Al-Awsat)
Amin H. Nasser, President & CEO of Aramco, has warned that the consequences of attacks on the region’s energy infrastructure extend beyond its own markets to economies and markets around the world. He stressed that protecting energy facilities and supply routes requires international cooperation, as recent disruptions pose challenges to global energy security.
Speaking at the 25th WPC Energy Congress in Riyadh, Nasser said disruptions in the Strait of Hormuz and attacks on energy infrastructure across the region had exposed the extent to which the links in the global supply chain are interconnected. He noted that continued disruptions were intensifying pressure on crude oil and refined product markets, particularly diesel.
He added that available commercial inventories now offer only a limited buffer against shocks, warning against relying on them as a permanent solution for energy security. He stressed that restoring the flow of supplies through the Strait of Hormuz and rebuilding market confidence remain essential to easing pressure across the energy supply chain.
Nasser explained that Saudi Arabia’s energy system had continued to operate with high reliability throughout the crisis, supported by strategic investments in infrastructure and operational capabilities, diversified export routes, integrated facilities, and digital protection systems.
Nasser stressed that resilience in the energy sector cannot be achieved through a single pipeline, facility, or technology. Rather, it requires an integrated system encompassing infrastructure, spare production capacity, multiple export routes, and the financial and human resources needed to maintain operations and investment during crises.
He added that Aramco, in coordination with the Ministry of Energy, was exploring additional crude oil export options, alongside expanding storage capacity outside the Kingdom and deploying advanced technologies to inspect facilities, accelerate maintenance and repair work, and limit damage.
He emphasized that the costs of investments needed to build resilience are borne by the entities that undertake them, while the benefits extend to economies, consumers, businesses, and investors. Avoiding disruptions, he noted, has economic value that deserves greater recognition, helping sustain the investments essential to energy security.
Nasser noted that Aramco’s In-Kingdom Total Value Add program (iktva), launched more than a decade ago, had helped establish a domestic supply base and strengthen the company’s ability to respond to exceptional circumstances.
He said that more than 90% of the materials used to restore operations over the past eight months had been sourced locally, enabling restoration work to be completed in hours and days rather than weeks and months.
He added that localizing supply chains is an important factor in strengthening preparedness for crises, while also supporting the domestic economy, developing national industries, and creating jobs.
Nasser stressed that building resilience requires sustained investment over many years, rather than short-term decisions. He also highlighted the importance of protecting the capabilities already developed and preparing for the Kingdom’s future needs.
Regarding the company’s future plans, Nasser said expanding gas production was a key pillar of Aramco’s strategy, with the company targeting an approximately 80% increase in sales gas production by 2030 compared with 2021 levels.
He added that gas expansion complements the National Renewable Energy Program, which includes solar and wind projects aimed at diversifying electricity generation sources and reducing emissions.
He explained that combining gas with renewable energy supports a more diversified and resilient electricity generation mix, helping improve resource utilization and meet the needs of the domestic economy. This is being pursued alongside the development of carbon capture, utilization and storage (CCUS), artificial intelligence, and advanced digital solutions.
He noted that these efforts align with the objectives of Saudi Vision 2030, particularly economic diversification, the development of local capabilities, industrial growth, job creation, and the establishment of a more sustainable energy system.
The Middle East: A Pillar of Global Energy Security
Nasser stressed that the Middle East remains a key pillar of global energy security, given its substantial share of proven oil reserves and spare production capacity. He noted that more than 90% of the world’s spare production capacity is concentrated in the region, according to his remarks.
He added that diversifying supply sources does not necessarily mean being able to diversify the geographical locations from which energy originates. He warned that attacks on the region’s infrastructure have repercussions for markets and economies worldwide.
He emphasized that protecting the energy facilities and transportation routes on which the global economy depends requires joint international efforts. No single country, regardless of its capabilities, can protect every element of the global supply system on its own.
Regarding the future of energy, Nasser said transition pathways would differ from one country to another according to their needs and economic circumstances, stressing that oil and gas would remain important components of the global energy mix.
He added that providing reliable and affordable energy is essential to supporting economic growth, creating jobs, and improving living standards, particularly in developing economies facing challenges in meeting growing energy demand.
He concluded by emphasizing the importance of building an integrated energy system combining oil, gas, and renewable energy, one that operates efficiently under normal conditions and can withstand pressure during crises. Such a system, he said, would strengthen energy security in Saudi Arabia and around the world while supporting future growth.

Miras: Energy Security, Sustainability, or Affordability Cannot Be Sacrificed
https://english.aawsat.com/business/5328593-miras-energy-security-sustainability-or-affordability-cannot-be-sacrificed
Miras: Energy Security, Sustainability, or Affordability Cannot Be Sacrificed
Pedro Miras addresses attendees as the 25th WPC kicks off in Riyadh (Asharq Al-Awsat)
10:21-12 October 2026 AD ـ 02 Jumada Al-Ula 1448 AH
10:12-12 October 2026 AD ـ 02 Jumada Al-Ula 1448 AH
10:21-12 October 2026 AD ـ 02 Jumada Al-Ula 1448 AH
10:12-12 October 2026 AD ـ 02 Jumada Al-Ula 1448 AH
Miras: Energy Security, Sustainability, or Affordability Cannot Be Sacrificed
Pedro Miras addresses attendees as the 25th WPC kicks off in Riyadh (Asharq Al-Awsat)
Pedro Miras, President of WPC Energy, affirmed that the world needs more energy from multiple sources to keep pace with population and economic growth, emphasizing the necessity of balancing supply security, affordability, sustainability, and economic development, without sacrificing any of these priorities.
In his speech at the opening of the 25th WPC in Riyadh, Miras stated that Riyadh is the most suitable place to host the conference, noting the pivotal role Saudi Arabia plays in the global energy system, and its continued leadership in investments, innovation, and technological advancement in the sector.
He added that the conference, which gathers government and industry leaders, academics, technology experts, financiers, and international institutions over four days, serves as a platform to discuss ways to meet the world's increasing energy needs, while supporting prosperity, sustainability, and security for future generations.
He pointed out that the world is undergoing a period of profound transformations, significant challenges, and exceptional opportunities, with the global population expected to approach 10 billion by mid-century, accompanied by increased energy demand resulting from population growth, urbanization, and improved living standards.
He explained that energy consumption and human development have gone hand-in-hand throughout modern history, as the availability of energy has contributed to improving health, education, and living standards, emphasizing that continued human progress requires more energy.
He noted that oil and gas still provide a significant share of the world's energy needs, despite decades of innovation and growth in new technologies, considering this an affirmation of the scale of the upcoming challenge and the need to utilize multiple energy sources.
Miras stressed that the challenge is no longer about choosing between energy security, affordability, and sustainability, but rather about achieving progress in these areas in parallel with economic development.
He stated that billions of people around the world aspire to improve their living conditions, access better health and education services, and broader economic opportunities, all of which require reliable energy availability and continued investment in its system.
He added that countries differ in their circumstances, resources, priorities, and developmental stages, which necessitates adopting diverse paths to achieve the future of energy and utilizing a wide range of technologies and solutions, noting that this diversity represents a source of strength.
He affirmed that technological neutrality remains one of the fundamental principles of the WPC, allowing various energy sources and technologies to contribute to building a secure, affordable, and sustainable system.
Innovation Alone Is Not Enough Without Investment and Partnerships
Regarding technological developments, Miras said that artificial intelligence, digitalization, advanced materials, new fuels, hydrogen, and carbon capture, utilization, and storage technologies, along with other technologies, are reshaping the global energy landscape.
However, he stressed that technology alone is not enough to achieve the desired transformation, as it requires effective policies, stable and predictable regulatory frameworks, long-term investments, and enhanced international cooperation.
He explained that energy security remains essential for economic growth, social stability, and human well-being, and that a successful energy sector transformation cannot be achieved without ensuring supply security. Furthermore, the long-term sustainability of this security requires sufficient investments across various components of the energy system.
He added that no single country, company, or technology can address global energy challenges alone, emphasizing the importance of partnerships among governments, industry, academia, and international institutions.
Miras expressed his appreciation to the Kingdom, the Minister of Energy, and the Saudi Minister of Industry and Mineral Resources, Prince Abdulaziz bin Salman, for their efforts in organizing and hosting the conference in Riyadh.
He stated that for over 90 years, the WPC has provided a platform bringing together governments, industry, academia, and international institutions to exchange ideas, deepen understanding, and explore practical solutions for sector challenges.
He noted that the importance of dialogue and cooperation is increasing amid the uncertainty and fragmentation the world is experiencing, expressing his hope that the conference discussions will contribute to developing partnerships and exploring innovations that will shape the future of energy.
He concluded by affirming that the goal is to build an energy future that leaves no one behind, achieving progress in supply security, affordability, sustainability, and economic development, based on innovation, cooperation, and mutual respect.

### 15. ★★★☆☆ Fed Chair Kevin Warsh and the FOMC Just Fired a Warning Shot at the Second-Priciest Stock Market in History - The Motley Fool

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: GN Fed / GN Markets
- **多源确认**: 2
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T09:06:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Fed Chair Kevin Warsh and the FOMC Just Fired a Warning Shot at the Second-Priciest Stock Market in History&nbsp;&nbsp;The Motl

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

This has been a year highlighted by a changing of the guard. Longtime CEOs Warren Buffett and Tim Cook handed over the keys to their respective successors, and President Donald Trump&#x27;s Fed chair nominee, Kevin Warsh, became only the 17th head of the central bank on May 22.
Despite these changes, Wall Street&#x27;s preeminent stock indexes, the Dow Jones Industrial Average ( ^DJI +0.17% ) , S&P 500 ( ^GSPC +0.02% ) , and Nasdaq Composite ( ^IXIC -0.15% ) have been unstoppable. The artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure build-out and otherworldly growth prospects accompanying this game-changing innovation have lifted the broader market to new heights.
This won&#x27;t be a one-and-done rate-hiking cycle. Image source: Official Federal Reserve Photo.
But sustaining this AI-driven bull market could be easier said than done. Last week, Fed Chair Warsh and his Federal Open Market Committee (FOMC) colleagues fired an unmistakable warning shot at the second-priciest stock market in history. The only question is: How many investors are paying attention?
The Federal Reserve&#x27;s rate-hiking cycle is ramping up
On Sept. 16, Warsh and 11 other voting members of the FOMC unanimously raised the federal funds target rate by 25 basis points to 3.75%-4.00%. It&#x27;s just the fourth time this century that the central bank has kicked off a rate-hiking cycle .
Warsh and the FOMC have been compelled to act because of persistently elevated inflation. August marked the 66th consecutive month in which the annualized inflation rate topped the FOMC&#x27;s long-term 2% target.
"The plain fact is that inflation is too high and has been for too long," https://t.co/htRQqyQHtr
While rising prices are perfectly normal in an expanding economy, inflation has soared in 2026 due to Trumpflation (inflation that&#x27;s specifically driven by President Trump&#x27;s policies). Donald Trump&#x27;s tariffs are increasing domestic manufacturing costs, while the Iran war has led to the ongoing closure of the Strait of Hormuz. The largest energy supply disruption in modern history has sent fuel prices soaring.
The newly released Federal Reserve meeting minutes (effectively a summary of what policymakers discussed behind closed doors on Sept. 15-16) point to tariffs, the Iran war, and the AI infrastructure build-out as key sources of inflation.
More importantly, the Fed meeting minutes charted a path forward for interest rates. Contained in the Fed minutes was this statement:
With regard to the outlook for monetary policy beyond this current meeting, most participants assessed that another increase in the target range for the federal funds rate would likely be appropriate by year end. While this statement doesn&#x27;t tell us whether the FOMC would raise interest rates at the October or December meeting, it makes it crystal clear that additional rate hikes are expected in the not-too-distant future. That&#x27;s terrible news for a historically expensive stock market.
Stock Market Shiller PE Ratio on the verge of taking out its Dot Com Bubble all-time high 🚨 🤯 👀 https://t.co/CtCmSgWnLt
A truly jaw-dropping amount of capital is being spent on the AI data center build-out , part of which has been financed with debt. If lending costs rise, as the FOMC&#x27;s September meeting minutes imply, it could slow this expansion and adversely impact lofty growth expectations.
In June, the S&P 500&#x27;s Shiller Price-to-Earnings Ratio came within a stone&#x27;s throw of surpassing the priciest valuation multiple in 156 years , achieved just before the dot-com bubble burst. This is a stock market with little to no margin for error. If the Fed&#x27;s rate-hiking cycle ramps up, maintaining this aggressive AI build-out without any "hiccups" would likely prove impossible.

### 18. ★★★☆☆ Microsoft to rely more on homegrown AI chips, reducing dependence on Nvidia - CNBC

Microsoft will ramp up use of its homegrown artificial intelligence chips after years of reliance on Nvidia&#x27;s

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Microsoft will mainly use its Maia chips for AI inference
Microsoft said it will eventually have multiple gigawatts worth of its Maia AI chips.
The company intends to make Maia its primary architecture for running AI models.
The next-generation Maia 300 chip enters production in 2027.
Microsoft will ramp up use of its homegrown artificial intelligence chips after years of reliance on Nvidia&#x27;s graphics processing units, as the software giant attempts to bolster its AI stack.
Starting with its third-generation Maia AI accelerator, unveiled on Monday, Microsoft said it will mainly rely on its own silicon to handle incoming requests.
Microsoft&#x27;s greatest strength in AI has been its Azure cloud, which runs a series of models for clients. Its Microsoft 365 Copilot and GitHub Copilot offer AI-powered services to software engineers and other office workers. And the company develops in-house models, though they&#x27;ve yet to make a big splash in a market dominated by OpenAI , Anthropic and Google .
The emerging opportunity in chips stems from unrelenting demand for advanced processors and a desire in the market for choice. Nvidia&#x27;s GPUs have been the standard for AI workloads and models, and they fill Microsoft&#x27;s existing data centers, while also serving to train models from OpenAI and Anthropic.
But as demand keeps growing, the model leaders have embraced a variety of silicon from companies including Amazon , Advanced Micro Devices , Cerebras , Google and, in recent months, Microsoft.
For general-purpose use, Microsoft already taps its own central processing units as well as silicon from Intel and AMD . Finance chief Amy Hood said at a Goldman Sachs conference last month that use of its own chips has helped with "margin improvement over the course of a decade."
Microsoft introduced its inaugural AI chip, the Maia 100, in 2023 . It touted the Maia 200 this past January , and CEO Satya Nadella told analysts in July that Maia 200 "delivers 30% better performance per dollar than the latest generation hardware in our fleet" and said it was handling OpenAI models.
Production of the Maia 300 starts in 2027. Microsoft is targeting multi-gigawatt deployments of chips in the Maia family, according to a blog post from Executive Vice President Scott Guthrie. But Alistair Speirs, a general manager, declined to provide a timeline.
"We reserve a little bit of optionality there, because this is an industry that&#x27;s changing very fast, and we want to deploy the right silicon for the right models that we&#x27;re using at the right time as well," Speirs said.
AI&#x27;s quiet safety gatekeepers are stepping into the spotlight
Microsoft&#x27;s Nadella says AI needs an &#x27;emergency brake&#x27; that humans control
Nvidia GPUs are everywhere. Here are the ways companies are accessing them
Hollywood takes on Zuckerberg, Musk and Altman amid widespread anxiety over AI
Microsoft expects to pack 96 Maia 300 chips into each data center rack, double the number for the Maia 200. The new version can run through up to seven times more AI tokens per rack, with 40% more tokens per megawatt when compared with GPUs.
"We need new architectures that will give us essentially better performance, better use of the power that we have," Speirs said. "You can think of power translating pretty directly into operational cost for something like a data center as well."
Parts of Microsoft 365 Copilot should speed up with Maia 300, said Jim Kleewein, a technical fellow at the company.
In a note distributed to clients on Wednesday, Evercore ISI analysts estimated that Maia will represent about 10% of Microsoft&#x27;s spending on AI chips by the first quarter of 2027. The analysts recommend buying Microsoft shares.
Nvidia will still be a big part of the equation. CEO Jensen Huang spoke at an event last week where Microsoft announced a Surface laptop containing an Nvidia processor. And when it comes to training models, Microsoft partners with Nvidia and AMD, Speirs said.
Unlike Google&#x27;s tensor processing units or Amazon&#x27;s Inferentia and Trainium, Microsoft&#x27;s accelerators can&#x27;t be rented by the hour to other companies.
"Nobody has tested them outside of Microsoft yet," said Patrick Moorhead, CEO of advisory firm Moor Insights and Strategy.

First Thing: Zelenskyy blindsided after Trump announces ‘energy ceasefire’ in Ukraine war
Ukrainian president says the deal is ‘news to me’ and that Russia is still attacking energy infrastructure. Plus, investigation into continuing mystery of Havana syndrome
Don’t already get First Thing in your inbox? Sign up here
Good morning. The Ukrainian president, Volodymyr Zelenskyy, has said it was “news to me” after Donald Trump announced a partial ceasefire deal in the Ukraine war. The US president had posted to social media to say: “Effective immediately, there is an ENERGY CEASEFIRE in the War between Russia and Ukraine. Both parties have agreed.”
There was no sign that the Kremlin was observing Trump’s ceasefire. According to Zelenskyy, Russian drones took to the sky “throughout the day” on Sunday and there were several airstrikes, including on energy infrastructure. He said Russia hit a power station in Kharkiv – the latest of more than 200 strikes during the past week against electricity generation and transmission facilities in Ukraine.
How has the prospect of a deal been welcomed? Despite the unconventional nature of the announcement, the ceasefire appeared to garner support from Ukraine’s key western allies. The French president, Emmanuel Macron, said Paris supported a “real energy ceasefire” and the UK prime minister, Andy Burnham, said a sustained energy ceasefire was “the best route to get energy and fuel back into world markets”.
Will Trump’s deal with Putin bring down gas prices? The agreement to temporarily lift sanctions on US imports of Russian diesel will have little “meaningful” effect on motorists and businesses in the US and overseas, energy and foreign policy analysts have predicted .
Abdul El-Sayed says Democrats must ‘win by massive numbers’ to fend off Trump election denial
Abdul El-Sayed said Donald Trump and the president’s Republicans allies were ‘bound to try to sow doubt about the integrity of the results’. Photograph: Julia Demaree Nikhinson/AP Democrats must “win by massive numbers” during November’s federal midterm elections because Donald Trump and his Republican allies are bound to try to sow doubt about the integrity of the results, the Democratic Senate candidate Abdul El-Sayed of Michigan said on Sunday .
“Donald Trump has already threatened to do what he did in 2020, which is deny the outcomes of an election,” El-Sayed said. “So, yes, we have to win by massive numbers.”
Riyadh airport hit again after deadly Houthi missile attack, Saudi authorities say
An unidentified projectile hit terminal 4 of King Khalid airport, killing one person and injuring 11 others on Sunday. Photograph: Social Media/Reuters A projectile hit a passenger terminal at the airport in Riyadh on Sunday, the day after a deadly missile attack on the facility carried out by Yemen’s Iran-backed Houthi rebel group killed 13 people. An unidentified projectile hit terminal 4 at King Khalid international airport, starting a fire, killing one person and injuring 11, according to Saudi civil aviation authorities.
The escalating attacks threatened to draw more countries into the Yemen conflict and further widen the regional fallout of the US-Iran war, which has already spread to Lebanon and Iraq.
AI less regulated than milk, Microsoft president says in warning over safety
Brad Smith said AI could be a ‘tool and a weapon’ that should be regulated urgently. Photograph: Yves Herman/Reuters Artificial intelligence is less regulated than milk and washing machines, the president of Microsoft has said , as the tech industry warns over the safety of AI. Brad Smith said there should be more safety rules to govern the technology including a legal obligation to a “victim” if their system was breached by rogue AI agents.
There are growing calls from experts, politicians and regulators for tougher rules to control AI, as well as warnings from Anthropic, Google DeepMind and OpenAI that the pace of the technology’s development should slow. Smith said AI could be a “tool and a weapon” that should be regulated urgently.
US maternity wards face wave of closures as Medicaid cuts kick in
According to analysis by Protect Our Care, 52 maternity wards have closed since the HR1 bill was announced in July 2025. Photograph: Shannon Stapleton/Reuters Maternity wards across the US are facing a wave of closures as cuts to federal Medicaid funding take effect, experts have said. According to analysis by the nonprofit Protect Our Care, 52 maternity wards have closed since the HR1 bill was announced in July 2025, with a further eight having announced their upcoming closure.
The legislation, which comes into full effect in the new year, will result in many adults covered through Medicaid having to meet new work requirements and being subject to more frequent eligibility checks, while there will be less federal funding for states for Medicaid.
John Fetterman was a Democratic star when he was elected to the Senate in 2022, but has split with Democrats on a range of issues. Photograph: Kylie Cooper/Reuters
Donald Trump has openly encouraged Democratic US senator John Fetterman to switch parties and become a Republican just weeks before the federal midterm elections.
An ICE agent struck a man with a vehicle during a pursuit in Texas. Miguel Parra-Hernandez suffered a broken leg and was taken to a hospital after being hit by car during an ICE operation.
Republican Christopher Paul, accused of child rape, is on course to become police chief in small Louisiana town. He is running unopposed for the seat despite allegations he raped two girls 20 years ago.
The Beach Boys’ Mike Love has returned to the stage , aged 85, despite being described as “facing serious health challenges”.

<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Iran’s Revolutionary Guard Navy says oil tanker struck mine in Strait of Hormuz
Iran’s Revolutionary Guard Navy says oil tanker struck mine in Strait of Hormuz
IRGC Navy says explosion struck engine room of supertanker capable of carrying 2 million barrels of oil
ISTANBUL An oil tanker struck a mine while attempting to cross the Strait of Hormuz, triggering an explosion in its engine room, Iran’s Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC) Navy said Sunday.
The IRGC Navy said in a statement that the supertanker, capable of carrying around 2 million barrels of oil, was attempting to transit the strategic waterway with its navigation system switched off.
The oil tanker is the third vessel to strike naval mines along the prohibited route in the Strait of Hormuz since Saturday, it added.
The statement did not identify the vessel or specify its nationality or flag.
The IRGC Navy renewed its warning that vessels switching off their navigation systems while passing through the waterway would be classified as “hostile vessels.”
The announcement came after the US Central Command (CENTCOM) said in late August that international shipping lanes in the Strait of Hormuz had been reopened following the removal of Iranian naval mines by US forces.
Iran announced on July 12 that it had again closed the strait following the resumption of US attacks on July 8.
Tehran has threatened to target vessels using routes other than those designated by Iranian authorities or attempting to cross the waterway without permission.
The US and Israel launched a war against Iran on Feb. 28. Tehran responded with missile and drone attacks across the region and effectively closed the Strait of Hormuz, while Washington later imposed a naval blockade on Iranian ports.
The US and Iran subsequently signed a memorandum of understanding in June concerning freedom of navigation in the strait, but implementation stalled over disagreements related to security in the strategic waterway.

### 26. ★★★☆☆ Stock Market Warning Signal Seen Only 5 Times Since 1871 Points to Serious Downside Risk - The Motley Fool

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: GN Markets
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T14:13:04+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Stock Market Warning Signal Seen Only 5 Times Since 1871 Points to Serious Downside Risk&nbsp;&nbsp;The Motley Fool

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Is now a good time to invest in stocks? Or is the stock market overheated, overpriced, and due for a big sell-off? The answer depends on your time horizon and overall financial goals. But some investors are worried about a widely respected valuation metric called the Shiller CAPE ratio. This metric keeps track of cyclically adjusted price-to-earnings for the S&P 500 index ( ^GSPC +0.02% ) .
The Shiller CAPE ratio uses stock market data going back to 1871. During the past 155 years, it has only peaked above 30 on five occasions. Several of those peaks happened right before big stock market downturns:
1999 -- right before the bursting of the dot-com bubble
As of this writing, the Shiller CAPE ratio had reached 41.8. That&#x27;s its second-highest level of all time, higher than any time since 1999. Does this mean stocks are due for a crash ?
Not necessarily. No single valuation metric is 100% correct, and no way of looking at the stock market has a perfect track record of predicting the future. The S&P 500 moves up and down for all kinds of complex reasons.
The Shiller CAPE ratio is worth knowing about. But it doesn&#x27;t mean you have to make big changes in your investment allocations. Let&#x27;s see what the Shiller CAPE ratio could mean for your stock portfolio.
The Shiller CAPE ratio is well-respected by stock market analysts because it&#x27;s a way to compare the S&P 500 index&#x27;s valuation across long-term time frames. The CAPE ratio effectively "smooths out" the ups and downs of S&P 500 earnings due to changes in the business cycle, such as recessions, and adjusts company earnings per share for inflation.
However, there are a few reasons why the historical data-based approach of the CAPE ratio might not be the best way to predict future performance of the S&P 500 today. The CAPE ratio doesn&#x27;t include stock buybacks. It also uses Generally Accepted Accounting Principles (GAAP) earnings, and GAAP standards have changed a lot since 1871. Another big limitation of the CAPE ratio is that it looks at S&P 500 performance during the past 10 years -- but today&#x27;s stock prices are forward-looking, based on expectations of future earnings.
Should you keep buying the S&P 500 with a high CAPE ratio?
Ever since July 2009, the CAPE ratio has been higher than its long-term average of 17. But rather than delivering weak returns (which a high CAPE ratio would seem to suggest), the stock market has delivered strong returns during that time.
For example, in the past 16 years since September 2010, the Vanguard S&P 500 ETF ( VOO +0.03% ) has delivered annualized returns of about 15%. That&#x27;s much better than the S&P 500 long-term average of 10% annualized returns.
It&#x27;s possible that the S&P 500 has reached a "new normal" regarding the CAPE ratio. Today&#x27;s S&P 500 companies might just be more profitable and have stronger earnings potential compared to previous eras. The economy is a lot bigger than it was in 1871. 58% of Americans own stocks today, while in 1871, buying stocks was more of a pastime for only the richest people.
Is the Shiller CAPE ratio irrelevant or outdated? No. It can be a good tool, and it&#x27;s worth watching. But don&#x27;t assume that this one imperfect valuation method is 100% correct at predicting the future.

### 27. ★★★☆☆ Stock market today: Dow, S&P 500, Nasdaq futures rise in lead up to earnings season, oil prices fall - AOL.com

US stocks rose on Monday morning as investors geared up for big bank earnings on Tuesday and watched energy markets for geopolitical developments.
The Dow Jones Industrial Average ( ^DJI ) rose 0.3%, while the S&P 500 ( ^GSPC ) added 0.1%. The tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite ( ^IXIC ) fell 0.1% after a winning week on Wall Street that saw the S&P 500 and Nasdaq post record highs.
Bond markets are closed for Columbus Day, also observed as Indigenous Peoples Day.
The bull market turned four on Monday, with many strategists expecting more room for stocks to run . However, concerns over an AI bubble , elevated Treasury yields, and geopolitical uncertainty present hurdles.
Investors expect corporate America to deliver another quarter of robust earnings growth, and that optimism will  face its first test on Tuesday  when several major banks, including JPMorgan ( JPM ), Goldman Sachs ( GS ), Citi ( C ), and Wells Fargo ( WFC ), report results.
Oil prices edged higher, with Brent crude futures ( BZ=F ) rising to $106 per barrel and WTI crude ( CL=F ) up to $93 per barrel, after Russia struck Ukrainian power plants. The attacks came after President Trump said Russia and Ukraine agreed to refrain from targeting each other&#x27;s energy infrastructure.
At the same time, Trump suggested the US may join Saudi Arabia in attacks on the Houthis following Houthi strikes on Saudi Arabia&#x27;s main airport, which killed 12 over the weekend. Such a move would risk escalating the war in the Middle East.
While no economic data is slated for release on Monday, key inflation and retail sales data will be released on Wednesday and Thursday.

### 31. ★★★☆☆ India’s retail inflation jumps to 5.54% in September, above estimates

- **分类**: 💰 全球金融
- **来源**: CNBC Top
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T10:49:24+00:00
- **发生了什么**: India inflations jumps for the 11-straight month, raising prospects of a further interest rate hike

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

India’s retail inflation jumps to 5.54% in September, above estimates
India’s consumer price inflation increased to 5.54% in September from 4.82% in August.
India&#x27;s central bank expects core inflation to be at 4.4% for the financial year ending March 2027 and headline inflation at 5.2%, higher than its medium-term target of 4%.
Shoppers gather at New Market in Kolkata, India, on September 21, 2026, ahead of the Durga Puja festival.
India&#x27;s headline inflation rose to 5.54% in September from 4.82% in August , adding pressure on the country&#x27;s central bank to further tighten monetary policy.
The consumer price index print was slightly above economists&#x27; expectations of 5.40%, according to a Reuters poll. Inflation has been on the rise for 11 straight months in the world&#x27;s fastest-growing economy and has touched a 22-month high in September.
India&#x27;s food inflation climbed 7.74% in September from 5.97% the month prior, with prices rising faster in rural areas than cities, India&#x27;s Ministry of Statistics and Program Implementation said in a Monday release. Transport services for goods inflation rose 14.73% while that for personal transport jumped 7.46%, as per the release.
Earlier this month, the Reserve Bank of India raised its interest rates for the first time since 2023 , joining several major central banks in tightening monetary policy in a bid to arrest accelerating inflation. India&#x27;s central bank also said that in the "current conditions" further rate cuts were "off the table in the near term" and policy action could either be a rate hike or a pause.
India is among the countries most  vulnerable to the supply disruptions  caused by the Iran war. It imports nearly 85% of its fuel needs, and before the Iran war it heavily relied on energy supplies through the  Strait of Hormuz .
Oil prices have been elevated due to the tensions in the Middle East. Experts, however, predict that prices will remain volatile in the near term amid concerns over the Middle East conflict and the impact of Hurricane Isaias.
India&#x27;s central bank expects core inflation to be at 4.4% for the financial year ending March 2027 and headline inflation at 5.2%, which is higher than the RBI&#x27;s medium-term target of 4%.
The RBI raised India&#x27;s economic growth estimate by 40 basis points to 7.1% but cautioned that protracted geopolitical tensions, trade frictions, tightening of financial conditions and elevated international commodity prices would likely weigh on economic activity.
Food prices in particular and India&#x27;s rural economy also face an added risk from the Super El Nino , with the country receiving its lowest rainfall in over a decade during June to September, a key season for its largely rain-fed farms and reservoirs.
The rainfall was the fourth lowest since 2001, and the 13th driest since 1901, according to the India Meteorological Department. Several parts of key farm states such as Maharashtra, Andhra Pradesh, Punjab, and Haryana are facing extreme drought conditions.
HSBC said in a report on Oct. 7 that it expects another rate hike in December, a day after the RBI policy action. "This may not be a deep tightening cycle, but a tightening cycle it is," the analysts said, adding that the September retail inflation data is likely to show a "sizeable headline pop."

</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

A warm day in Žabljak, Montenegro. Photograph: Alamy
A warm day in Žabljak, Montenegro. Photograph: Alamy
Balkan temperatures swing as heavy rain heads for Greece
Clear skies bring warm days and cold nights to Montenegro, while a low-pressure system moves towards Aegean Sea
Temperatures fluctuated dramatically in the Balkans last week, with unusually large differences between daytime highs and overnight lows – known as the diurnal range.
An area of high pressure over the region brought prolonged sunshine during the day, while clear skies at night allowed temperatures to drop rapidly as heat escaped into the atmosphere without cloud cover to slow the cooling.
In Kosanica, Montenegro , the average maximum temperature in October is 15C (59F), while the average minimum is 4C. However, on 4 and 5 October the difference between daytime highs and overnight lows reached 30C, more than double the October average.
Elsewhere in Europe , high pressure will build across the north and west over the coming week, pushing an area of low pressure southwards towards the Mediterranean. The low-pressure system will deepen as it crosses the Adriatic and Ionian seas before moving eastwards into the Aegean Sea by the middle of the week.
Spells of heavy rain and strong winds are expected to sweep across Greece as a result. Forecast models suggest widespread rainfall of 50-150mm, with the highest totals expected around the island of Skiathos.
Towards the end of the week, the system will continue eastwards, bringing showers to parts of Crete, which was hit by severe flooding a week earlier. Those storms swept some cars and trees into the sea.
People cross a bridge over a swollen river as Hurricane Simon approaches Mexico’s Pacific coast in Costalegre, Jalisco state. Photograph: Daniel Becerril/Reuters Meanwhile, Hurricane Simon, a category 4 storm, is moving close to Mexico’s Pacific coast, near the state of Jalisco. It is the latest of 21 named storms in the Pacific this season, 11 of which have reached hurricane strength.
At the time of writing, Simon has a central pressure of 933mb and maximum sustained winds of nearly 150mph (241km/h). However, the hurricane is expected to weaken rapidly if its core moves onshore, cutting it off from the warm ocean waters that fuel it.
Heavy rainfall is affecting south-western Mexico, where totals could reach 300-400mm across a wide area, rising to 400-500mm in the wettest locations.

</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

VW is exhibiting at the Paris show for the first time in 20 years, unveiling its new all-electric ID Tiguan. Photograph: Volkswagen AG
VW is exhibiting at the Paris show for the first time in 20 years, unveiling its new all-electric ID Tiguan. Photograph: Volkswagen AG
Volkswagen boss backs EU plan to bolster domestic European industry
Oliver Blume says ‘Made in Europe’ rules to counter rapid rise of lower-price Chinese brands ‘must reward real value creation’
The boss of Volkswagen has backed EU proposals to boost its domestic industry, arguing European carmakers need to be able to “compete under comparable conditions” with Chinese rivals.
Oliver Blume said “Made in Europe” rules from the EU “must reward real value creation in Europe”, speaking in Paris before the motor show this week.
The rules are aimed at countering the rapid rise of Chinese brands in Europe in the automotive sector and other important manufacturing industries. Lower-priced Chinese manufacturers have prompted a crisis in several industries.
Volkswagen is among the most-affected European companies. Germany’s biggest carmaker has launched a brutal plan to cut as many as 100,000 jobs . Blume said it was the “largest transformation programme in its history”.
Alarm bells sound in Brussels as EU sales of Chinese hybrid cars rocket
The proposals for the Made in Europe rules, formally known as the Industrial Accelerator Act, would limit subsidies and public procurement to products with a large proportion of their materials and manufacturing coming from within the EU.
Blume listed a litany of challenges facing European carmakers, saying: “Europe is under enormous competitive pressure.” These included: high energy costs; lower demand as consumers struggle with high inflation; the need to be quicker to develop cars; and intense competition from China .
On Friday, the EU said it had reached a landmark deal with China to “halve” its sales of hybrid cars in the bloc amid fears surging sales could kill off parts of the European car industry.
Blume said Germany’s carmakers were happy to compete with Chinese rivals, but said the EU should try to reward businesses with significant European footprints.
“Companies that invest and develop in Europe must see a clear benefit,” he said.
Referring to Chinese companies, he added: “Those who sell here should compete under comparable conditions and create jobs and value here in Europe, too.
The new all-electric ID Tiguan. Photograph: Volkswagen AG Blume added: “We believe in open markets, but openness also means standing up for our own interests in Europe. Because a strong industrial base means more than economic success. It protects technological independence.”
Volkswagen is exhibiting at the Paris show for the first time in 20 years. Blume emphasised the “close ties between France and Germany”, while hosting the French industry minister, Sébastian Martin.
Arno Antlitz, VW’s chief financial officer, said the job cuts were necessary to “increase our economic power” by increasing profitability and allowing it to defend its “home turf”.
The company is cutting the number of models it offers worldwide from 150 to 75 across brands including Audi and Škoda. It hopes the move will reduce complexity in its factories and for dealers, cutting the cost of each vehicle it makes.
On Sunday, it revealed the production version of its ID Tiguan, an electric edition of its bestselling family SUV.

Stock Market Today: S&P 500, Dow Futures Gain, Nasdaq 100 Falls as Iran Flags ‘Inadequate’ US Response to Peace Terms — SMP, QMCO, SVRN in Focus (UPDATED)
Editor’s Note: The ETFs tracking benchmark indices and the headline were updated in the story.
U.S. stock futures were mixed on Monday, as the Dow Jones and S&P 500 indices rose, while the Nasdaq 100 index fell, following Friday’s higher close.
Geopolitical tensions flared over the weekend as Iran stepped up attacks on shipping near the Strait of Hormuz and threatened regional digital infrastructure, citing an “inadequate” U.S. response to its peace terms. The escalation comes amid reports that Tehran rejected Washington’s latest proposals to end the conflict, with prediction markets signaling low odds of a near-term diplomatic resolution to reopen the strategic waterway.
Attention on Wall Street is turning toward third-quarter earnings reports. Several major financial heavyweights—such as JPMorgan Chase & Co. (NYSE: JPM ), Bank of America Corp. (NYSE: BAC ), Citigroup Inc. (NYSE: C ), Wells Fargo & Company (NYSE: WFC ), Morgan Stanley (NYSE: MS ), and Goldman Sachs Group Inc. (NYSE: GS )—are set to release their latest results this week.
The bond market is closed on Monday for Columbus Day, though the equity market remains open. Later this week, investors will digest September Consumer Price Index (CPI) inflation data, retail sales figures, and the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book.
Meanwhile, the 10-year Treasury bond yield stood at 5.24%, and the 2-year Treasury bond yield was at 4.79%, as of Friday’s close. The CME Group’s FedWatch tool projections show markets pricing in an 18.8% likelihood of the Federal Reserve hiking interest rates after its October meeting.
The SPDR S&P 500 ETF Trust (NYSE: SPY ) and Invesco QQQ Trust ETF (NASDAQ: QQQ ), which track the S&P 500 index and Nasdaq 100 index, respectively, were mixed in premarket trading on Monday. The SPY rose by 0.063% to $779.06, while the QQQ declined 0.13% to $750.27.
Will S&P 500 open higher? Polymarket odds reach 78% as Wall Street weighs Q3 bank earnings, cooling oil prices, and broad sector momentum.
Standard Motor Products Inc. (NYSE: SMP ) fell 3.14% in premarket trading on Monday as it named James J. Burke as interim CFO, effective Oct. 30, succeeding Nathan R. Iles.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that SMP maintains a weak price trend in the short, long, and medium term, with a moderate quality score.
Quantum Corp. (NASDAQ: QMCO ) was 2.71% lower in Monday premarket as it terminated William H. White as CFO and principal financial officer, effective Oct. 9, 2026. The company named Hiral A. Patel as principal financial officer.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that QMCO maintains a strong price trend in the long, short, and medium terms.
J-Star Holding Co. Ltd. (NASDAQ: YMAT ) surged 153.49% after the company announced its ongoing corporate capital initiatives will fund the planned U.S. expansion for drone frame manufacturing.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that YMAT maintains a weak price trend in the short, long, and medium terms.
SVRN Inc. (NASDAQ: SVRN ) jumped 15.89% after the company announced it acquired FastNEAR, a provider of Remote Procedure Call infrastructure that supports applications on the NEAR Protocol.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that SVRN maintains a strong price trend in the short, long, and medium terms.
First Tracks Biotherapeutics Inc. (NASDAQ: TRAX ) was 18.68% higher after it said that it will announce top-line data from Cohort 1 of Phase 1b trial of ANB033, a CD122 antagonist, in celiac disease on Oct. 12.
Benzinga’s Edge Stock Rankings indicate that TRAX maintains a strong price trend in the long term but a weak trend in the medium and short terms.
Scott Bessent Fires Back at Elizabeth Warren’s ‘Trumpflation’ Tracker — Treasury’s Counterattack Makes a Very Different Case About Trump’s Economy
Scott Bessent calls Elizabeth Warren an 'inflationista' while releasing the 'Trump Affordability Tracker' to contrast US economic data.
Real estate, consumer discretionary and health care stocks recorded the biggest gains, while communication services and energy bucked the overall market trend to close lower, as most S&P 500 sectors ended Friday positive.
In his analysis, Mohamed El-Erian characterizes the U.S. economy as fundamentally strong yet navigating an increasingly precarious environment. He notes that the economy remains robust—growing in the “3–4% range, according to the Atlanta Fed GDPNow measure”—supported by a “strong labor market.”
However, he cautions that this expansion coexists with significant fiscal headwinds, pointing out that the U.S. budget deficit reached “around 6% of GDP, despite a robust economy.”
Regarding the stock market, El-Erian observes that U.S. equity indices continue to reach “new record highs,” but warns that this growth is highly fragile, being “driven almost exclusively by narrow momentum in the technology sector.”
Looking forward, he highlights that the Federal Reserve is “navigating an increasingly narrow path” as it balances robust economic activity against inflationary pressures. While policymakers are threading a “tight needle” to maintain stability—making an October rate hike unlikely—upcoming CPI data could upend expectations.
El-Erian warns that a hotter-than-expected inflation print could trigger further fixed-income volatility and increase financial strain, as evidence already suggests “some leakage of interest-rate risk into credit risk.”
Here’s what investors will be keeping an eye on this week.
On Tuesday, September’s NFIB index of small business optimism data will be released by 6:00 a.m. ET.
September’s existing home sales data will be out by 10:00 a.m., and September’s monthly Treasury balance data will be released by 2:00 p.m. ET.
On Wednesday, September’s CPI, core CPI, annual CPI, and annual core CPI data will be released by 8:30 a.m. ET.
The Federal Reserve Beige Book will be released by 2:00 p.m. ET.
On Thursday, October’s Empire State manufacturing survey, September’s retail sales, October’s Philadelphia Fed business outlook survey, initial jobless claims for the week ending Oct. 10, September’s PPI, core PPI, and annual PPI data will all be released by 8:30 a.m. ET.
August’s manufacturing and trade inventories data will be out by 10:00 a.m. ET.
Federal Reserve Bank of Cleveland President Beth Hammack will speak at 4:30 p.m., and Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh will speak at 11:30 p.m. ET.
On Friday, September’s import prices data will be released by 8:30 a.m. ET.
September’s industrial production and capacity utilization data will be out by 9:15 a.m. ET.
Crude Oil WTI futures were trading higher in the early New York session by 0.79% to hover around $92.58 per barrel.
Gold Spot US Dollar fell 0.40% to hover around $4,177.47 per ounce. The U.S. Dollar Index spot was 0.1% higher at the 102.31 level.
Meanwhile, Bitcoin (CRYPTO: BTC) was trading 0.70% higher at $83,513.47 per coin over the last 24 hours.
Asian markets traded mixed on Monday, as Hong Kong’s Hang Seng and Australia’s S&P/ASX 200 indices rose. While China’s CSI 30, India’s Nifty 50, South Korea’s Kospi and Japan’s Nikkei 225 indices declined. European markets were mostly higher in early trade.
